So I keep doing it until that feeling stops, so I can move on, even if it makes me late.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about forgetting something or not doing something right feel intrusive and unwanted, or do they feel more like a normal part of your routine that you just want to double-check?\n\nPatient: Um... they're definitely unwanted. I don't... I don't want to be thinking about them. \n\nIt's just... these nagging thoughts keep popping up. I just want them to stop.\n\nDoctor: When you’re checking things like the stove or your aunt’s pills, do you feel like you’re trying to prevent something bad from happening, even if you know it might not make sense logically?\n\nPatient: Um... I just keep doing it until I feel calm enough to actually move on... even when I know it's making me run late. Just... making sure it's done.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that unsettled feeling before you check things, do you ever try to manage it by doing something else in your mind or body—like silently repeating a word, counting, or focusing on a specific sensation—to feel more in control?\n\nPatient: Um... no. That's... that's really it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other types of recurring thoughts or urges that bother you, or felt like you needed to do other things in a certain way, even if you couldn't explain why?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think so. I feel like I've already told you everything that bothers me. There really isn't anything else going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to arrange things in a certain way, make things symmetrical, or get something just right in your mind before you can move on, even if it's not about safety or forgetting something?\n\nPatient: No... not at all. I don't really care if things are perfectly lined up or... symmetrical. I don't do any of that. Like I said, it's really just what we already talked about. I don't have any of these other things you're asking about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
